### 2026-09-10 16:15 — the day's work committed on `workflow-audit-audio-picture-story`

Everything above landed in one commit: 107 modified files, 13 new (`gpu_telemetry.py`, the sfx
pack builders, the Mixkit licence research note, and six new test modules). The gates were re-run
first, because files were still being touched at 15:38 — three hours after the last recorded run —
and two gates had gone red in the meantime:

```
just fmt          -> 3 files reformatted (test_anchor_conditioning, test_prompt_compile,
                     test_release_gpu_when_idle), ruff check --fix: All checks passed
just lint         -> 0 errors (pre-existing NodeGraphEditor warnings only)
just typecheck    -> 8 errors, then 0. All eight were one wrong annotation:
                     `_packaged()` in tests/unit/test_gallery_publish.py returns a StageContext
                     and was declared `-> Path`, so pyright rejected every call that passed it
                     to stage_compile_destination_packages and every `.ddir()` on it.
just schemas      -> 70 schemas, 60 node types, 16 templates regenerated
just test         -> 0   1369 passed, 1 skipped, 55 deselected (5:47) + pnpm: 8 packages green
just test-integration -> 1 failed, 48 passed (4:24); the failure re-run alone: 3 passed (1:58)
```

The integration failure is **finding 107 again**, unchanged and not a new defect:
`test_stopping_a_producing_run_cancels_the_node_in_flight` reached `FAILED: activity Heartbeat
timeout` under load (average 3.0, another session rendering on the same box) and passes on its
own. Second sighting, which is worth saying out loud — a stop test that races a heartbeat deadline
will keep costing a re-run until the deadline is raised or the gate stops depending on wall-clock
progress.

`just schemas-check` reports "drift" for the four generated files right up until the commit: it
gates with `git status --porcelain`, so regenerated-but-uncommitted is indistinguishable from
stale to it. It goes green with the commit, which is the state CI checks.

### 2026-09-10 — the second host as a producer: bringing its work home

**The problem, stated correctly.** nova draws for this repo but produces nothing on its own disk:
HiDream is base64 in and base64 out, so its frames are written *here*. Verified — nova had no
`output/` directory at all. The moment it runs a whole lane, the stages that decide how a film ends
(post chain, MMAudio, TTS, Blender, ffmpeg — all absolute local paths) leave the finished work on
the wrong machine. The operator's decision: nova becomes a second producer, and **only the end
deliverable comes home**, pulled from here when a run finishes.

**What made it small.** `compile_destination_packages` already writes
`destination-packages/packages.json` naming every shippable file with role, deliverable-relative
path and **sha256**. So there is no new contract: the harvest reads a manifest a stage produced,
through `DeliveryPackage`. Two defences come free from parsing it as a contract rather than as
JSON — `DeliveryFile._relative` already refuses an absolute path or a `..` segment, and
`extra="forbid"` turns a host on a different commit into *reported skew* instead of a half
transfer.

**What shipped.** `content-factory remote {list,harvest}` and `just remote-list` / `just harvest`,
over `services/harvest.py`. One ssh probe per host (a stdlib-only script sent on stdin, so it works
against a worker that is a commit behind), then `rsync --files-from` of the manifest plus a few
hundred KB of evidence, every digest re-checked on arrival, an atomic rename into
`output/harvest/<host>/<slug>/`, and the film hard-linked into `videos/`. Off by default:
`CF__REMOTE__HOSTS` empty is byte for byte what this repo did before.

**No ledger.** "Already here" is derived from a `harvest.json` sidecar written *last*, so a
directory with bytes and no sidecar is a failed harvest that gets redone, and
`rm -rf output/harvest/nova/<slug>` is a complete undo. Identity is the digest of the manifest's
files, not the run name: `run-local` writes every run of a lane to the same project directory
unless told otherwise, so a name cannot say whether something is new.

**Measured, on a real pull from nova** (`o04-single-clip-post`, staged there and harvested back):

```
47 files / 8.0 MB on nova  ->  11 files / 3.7 MB here
sha256 of the harvested final.mp4 == the original run's, byte for byte
second pass: "already here", zero transfers
```

**Five things the tests did not catch and one real run did.**

* **`rsync --files-from` errors on a name it cannot stat** — exit 23, *after* transferring
  everything else. The evidence list is optimistic by design, so the first real harvest refused a
  run whose files had all arrived. `--ignore-missing-args` is mandatory. The test double had been
  more forgiving than rsync; it now returns exit 23 exactly as rsync does, and dropping the flag
  fails 13 tests.
* **A size check is not an identity check.** The gallery's "is this already the same film" compared
  byte counts. Two different films can be the same length; it compares digests now.
* **`rsync -a` preserves symlinks and sha256 follows them**, so a symlink that arrived would be
  hashed *through* — reading a local file and calling it harvested. `--safe-links` on the wire and
  a refusal before hashing here.
* **A file list is newline-delimited** and `DeliveryFile` permits a newline in a path. `--from0`.
* **The repo is on `/home` (465 GB free), not `/` (30 GB).** `df -h /` had been used to argue for a
  design decision. It did not change the decision; it did remove a wrong reason for it.

**The finding that matters most, and it is not comfortable.** `image-set` — nova's actual
workload — **never reaches a state this harvests**. `frames_gate` (`review_frames`) blocks between
`drift` and `package`, so a finished-looking image set ends with `passed: false` and no
`destination-packages/` at all. Six of the seven overnight image sets sit exactly there. So
`remote list` reports them as `awaiting review` rather than silently returning nothing, and the
round trip that would finish them — ship the frames and batch here, review, push `verdict.json`
back, resume `--from package` — is **designed and not built**. Anyone expecting image sets to flow
home today will get an honest "awaiting review" and no film.

`_publish_to_gallery` was not copied. The linking rule moved to `deliverables/gallery.py` and the
film-selection rule to `stages.primary_film`, both callers sharing one implementation; the six
existing gallery tests pass unchanged, which is the regression guard for the extraction.

**Commands run:**

```
just fmt / just lint                       -> 0 / 0
just typecheck                             -> 0 errors
just test                                  -> 0   1395 passed, 1 skipped, 55 deselected
just schemas-check                          -> {"templates": 16, "stale": []}  (no contract moved)
just test-integration                      -> 1 failed under load
                                              (test_run_stop::..._cancels_the_node_in_flight,
                                              finding 107 again), then **49 passed / exit 0** on a
                                              quiet box; the isolated file passed 3/3 in between
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_harvest.py   -> 26 passed
content-factory remote list (real nova)    -> {"runs": 0, "ready": 0}, then 1 ready once staged
content-factory remote harvest (real nova) -> 1 harvested, 3.8 MB, digests matched; 2nd pass 0
```

The staged run was removed from nova afterwards and `output/harvest/` deleted, so both hosts are
back where they started. `docs/gpu-hosts.md` gains "Part 3 — The second host as a producer, and
bringing the work home", including the four prerequisites (code parity, a real backend selection —
a stock `.env` runs every lane on **mock** and mock output still writes a valid manifest — card
ownership, and unique run names).

## Phase — "estimate the time its done ... represented actively in the workflow on the ui"

Every run already recorded `seconds` for every stage it executed. **240 `run.json` on disk, 225 of
them run reports carrying 1,161 timed stages across 39 stage names**, and nothing had ever read
them. So the estimate is not a table somebody wrote down: it is the median of what this machine
actually did. `services/durations.py` is that reader; `Estimate` carries `seconds` *and* `samples`,
because "about a minute" and "about a minute, from one run" are different claims and the number
alone cannot tell them apart.

**Median, not mean.** `generate_anchor` spans 31 s to 1,528 s under one name, because a lane that
draws one picture and a lane that draws six both call the stage that. One outlier drags a mean into
uselessness; the median ignores it.

**Two defects in my own estimator, both caught by checking its output against numbers I already
knew.**

* **Every lane-tagged report was counted twice** — samples were added to the per-lane bucket *and*
  the all-lanes bucket unconditionally, so 149 real `generate_anchor` samples read as 298. It was
  noticed because the number was *implausible*, not because a test failed.
* **The lane was never recorded.** `run_plan` accepted a `workflow` argument and did not write it
  into the report, so the per-lane keying the module was built around was inert: every lane got the
  same all-lanes median. `run_plan` writes it now.

**Recovering the lane from 225 reports that predate the field.** Throwing them away would have
meant estimating `audio-picture-story` from the median of 107 `single-image` runs: it read as
**"2m left" on a lane that takes half an hour**. A run executes a *contiguous slice* of its lane's
stage order, so that sequence names the lane — `infer_workflow` accepts a match only when exactly
one lane could have produced it.

```
181 of 225 attributed to one lane   44 ambiguous (short slices several lanes share)   0 unmatched
audio-picture-story  generate_anchor  195.0s n=8     single-image  generate_anchor  31.6s n=108
audio-picture-story  total: 2m left  ->  7m left, with own-lane history for 17 of its 18 stages
```

The 44 ambiguous fall back to the all-lanes median, which is the right answer to an ambiguous
question — putting a picture story's numbers on an image set would be worse than admitting the
question cannot be answered.

**What the estimate refuses to do**, each because the alternative is a number somebody would
believe:

* A **finished** node gets no estimate at all. It has a measured `duration_ms`; an estimate beside
  it is replacing a measurement with a guess.
* A **blocked** node gets none either, for a different reason: the stage has already run — it
  generated, checked its own output and handed the decision to a person — so its remaining cost is
  a human looking at it, and "~31 s" is the one answer certainly wrong. A run in which everything
  is blocked promises nothing at all, because it is not going to finish on its own.
* A running stage past its median reports **`overdue`, not a negative number** and not a re-added
  full median. "0 s left" on a stage still working is the honest reading, and the UI says "running
  longer than usual" rather than promising a finish that has passed.
* **Stages with no history are named, not guessed at** — counted as zero and listed, so the total
  says "plus 2 stage(s) never timed" instead of quietly under-promising.
* **Concurrent branches are not summed.** Several stages in flight run at once, so the run waits on
  whichever has the most left (max), while queued work is serial (sum). Summing the in-flight ones
  over-promises the finish on every DAG.
* Under a second the banner says **"Finishing now"**, with no clock time. `formatEta` returns
  "~any moment" for that range, and `"~any moment left"` is not English.
* Numbers **under a second are not printed as "0s"**. Most stages in a lane are bookkeeping, and
  `[~0s, 0s left]` trains the eye to skip the line where the real number is.

**One sentence, one place.** The server first sent a composed `label` alongside the numbers. The
browser localises the clock time and pluralises "past run(s)" itself, so that was two diverging
versions of one sentence; the payload carries numbers only. `Forecast.describe()` stays for the CLI.

**On screen.** The run view (`GET /v1/runs/{id}`, polled every 2 s, so it counts down live) gains
`eta` at the run level — remaining seconds, an aware `finish_at`, `samples`, `confident`, `overdue`,
`unknown_stages` — and `eta_seconds`/`eta_samples` per node. `RunDetailPage` shows a banner
("~11 min left · done about 14:32 · from 4 past runs"), each canvas node and step-list row carries
its own `~42 s` in italic so an estimate never looks like a measurement, and the inspector gains
"Still to go (median of 12)". The banner is `aria-live="polite"`; `eta` is **null**, not zero, once
nothing is unfinished.

**And for the runs I do myself**, which is where the question actually gets asked: the runner logs a
header, each stage line carries its own estimate and the countdown after it, and `ActiveRun` now
holds `eta_seconds` — so "how long until the card is free" is answerable from another terminal.
`make`'s terse formatter had been silently dropping any line it did not recognise, which is why the
first real run showed none of this.

```
single-image: 4 steps, 32s left, done about 17:30 (median of 108 past run(s))
ok   plan_story                   0.0s {"beats": 1, ...}  [32s left]
ok   generate_anchor              56.7s {"anchors": 1, "backend": "hidream-o1", ...}
```

That run is also the honest limit of this: **32 s estimated, 56.7 s actual**. The same lane at
different quality settings costs very differently and the reports do not record quality, so the
median sits between the cheap runs and the expensive ones. `samples` travels with every number for
exactly this reason. Keying on quality is the obvious next refinement and has no history behind it
yet.

**Gallery.** `ps2b-amber` is the first film to publish itself (`"gallery": "videos/ps2b-amber.mp4"`
in its own facts). `ps1c-pinecone` and `ps3c-whelk` finished before that change landed and were
backfilled through the same `publish_film` — hard links, `replace=False`, digests compared. 35
films in `videos/`. `ps2c-amber` is still parked at `review_frames`, stage 6 of 18.

**Commands run:**

```
just lint / just typecheck                 -> 0 errors (pre-existing a11y warnings only)
just test                                  -> exit 0   1413 passed, 1 skipped, 55 deselected
just test-integration                      -> exit 0   49 passed, 1420 deselected  (5m38s)
just schemas-check                         -> {"templates": 16, "stale": []}   no contract moved
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_run_eta.py   -> 19 passed
uv run pytest -m integration tests/api/test_runs_stop.py -> 3 passed (the route carries `eta`)
pnpm test (pipeline-canvas)                -> 16 passed
pnpm vitest run test/runs.test.tsx (web)   -> 10 passed
content-factory make single-image          -> exit 0, 56.7s, header + countdown as above (real GPU)
```

`tests/unit/test_run_eta.py` covers the double count (8 samples where 16 would have been), the lane
split (31 s vs 601 s under one stage name), the overdue floor, the concurrent-branch total, the
naive-timestamp read (SQLite returns no zone; reading it as local time made every node look two
hours old, i.e. an ETA of zero on everything), and the exact log lines a slow lane prints. `tests/api/test_runs_stop.py` adds the one link the
unit tests cannot reach: that `eta` survives the HTTP route the canvas actually polls.

The tracked fixture `fixtures/temporal/spike_production_run.history.json` is rewritten by every
`just test-integration` (fresh timestamps and task ids) — pre-existing churn, not from this work.

## Phase — "see the output images and videos and audio in the workflow, and the history of runs I've made"

**The gap.** `GET /v1/runs` reads the `production_runs` table, which holds durable Temporal runs —
on this machine 267 rows, 54 of them today from `cmp_wftest000001`, i.e. the integration suite.
Every film, drawing and narration actually produced here came from a **local** run
(`content-factory make`), which writes no database row at all. So **226 runs and 35 films** existed
on disk and none of it was reachable from the product that made it: `output/overnight/ps1c-pinecone/
deliverables/dlv_short0000001/exports/final.mp4` was the only way to watch a film.

`services/run_history.py` reads the reports that were already there. It is not a new source of
truth:

* **The packages manifest wins where it exists.** A run that reached
  `compile_destination_packages` wrote `destination-packages/packages.json` naming every
  deliverable file with its role, byte count and content type — that *is* what the run decided it
  produced, so its numbers are used over the ones on disk.
* **A run that never packaged still has outputs**, and here they are the ones that matter: most
  runs park at `review_frames` with the drawings finished. Those are found by scanning, and the
  path names them (`anchors/` a drawing, `exports/` a film, `controls/` a debug map).
* **Read-only.** It cannot cost a run anything.

**27 runs were reading as "failed" that were waiting for a person.** A human review gate raises
like any other stage failure and records neither `blocked` nor `blocked_at`, so every picture story
and image set parked at `review_frames` — drawings done, waiting for somebody to look — was
reported as a defect. `human_gate_stages()` now lives once in `dag_compiler` with three callers
(`make`'s GATE-vs-FAIL label, the history's outcome, the compiler's executor), and the history has
five outcomes rather than four:

```
complete 152   review 27   failed 36   stopped 7   blocked 4
```

**One containment rule, not two.** `api/files.py` holds resolve-and-contain: every check on the
*resolved* path (the case a string comparison misses is a symlink inside the tree pointing out of
it, and `output/` is full of hard links), an extension allowlist rather than a denylist, and **404
for everything** — telling "exists but forbidden" apart from "does not exist" hands the caller a
filesystem oracle. `/v1/sequences` was refactored onto it; its 26 tests pass unchanged, which is
the regression guard for the extraction.

**The panel.** A **History** button in the workspace toolbar opens three columns — lanes, the runs,
and the selected run's actual output. Media plays there rather than downloading, because the point
is to look at it: the session is a cookie, so `<img src>` and `<video src>` authenticate
themselves and the browser streams and caches, with no blob URLs and no JS holding a 6 MB film in
memory. `preload="metadata"` on video and `preload="none"` on audio, so a run with four films does
not pull 20 MB to draw a list, and the file route sets a one-year immutable `Cache-Control`
because a finished run does not rewrite its own frames.

**Measured on the real tree:**

```
list_runs                      226 runs in 466 ms   (one stat per report, no output scanning)
get_run overnight~ps1c-pinecone   265 files in 12 ms   film + 13 drawings + 32 audio + captions
get_run overnight~ps2c-amber      175 files in  8 ms   outcome=review, poster = its first anchor
```

**85 of pinecone's 125 JSON files were `<frame>.done.json` markers** — what the runner writes so a
stage can be re-run safely, not what the run produced. Listing those made a six-picture story a
list of 130 rows of bookkeeping, so they are classified `marker` and folded in with the control
maps and upscale intermediates: 265 files become **87 shown, 178 behind one toggle**, and the fold
is a labelled button rather than a filter, because a control map is exactly what you want when a
drawing came out wrong.

**Fixed on the way:**

* **"Why don't I see the workflows in the workspace?"** — not stale. All 16 lanes were in
  `workflowTemplates.ts` and `schemas-check` reported `{"templates": 16, "stale": []}`; the panel
  is behind the **Templates** toggle, which starts closed. The new History panel is a labelled
  toolbar button beside it for the same reason.
* The Job Queue's hint still said *"Workspace graphs are not runnable yet"* — the Run button
  beside it has been starting durable runs for a while. It now says what the panel holds and
  points local runs at History.
* `--cf-selected` did not exist. Only my own new CSS referenced it, so the selected history row
  would have had no highlight at all; the convention here is `--cf-accent-soft`.

**Investigated and deliberately not changed:** the integration suite writes its durable runs into
the *dev* database rather than `content_factory_test`, which is why `/projects` reads as noise.
That is by design — `tests/integration/test_workspace_graph_run.py` uses the app's own
`get_sessionmaker()` because it drives a real Temporal worker and has to share that worker's
connection. Rewiring it would mean overriding `DATABASE_URL` for the worker process, on a suite
that passes; recorded instead of risked.

**Commands run:**

```
just lint / just typecheck                 -> 0 errors (pre-existing a11y warnings only)
just test                                  -> exit 0
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_run_history.py            -> 8 passed
uv run pytest -m integration tests/api/test_run_history.py -> 5 passed (every traversal refused)
uv run pytest -m integration tests/api/test_runs_stop.py   -> 3 passed (sequences refactor safe)
pnpm vitest run test/history.test.tsx (web)             -> 6 passed
```

The API tests prove the half that must not be wrong: `../../../etc/passwd`, a URL-encoded
traversal, a symlink out of the output tree, a `..` walk through a real file, `.sh` and
`.safetensors` inside a run, and an id naming `..`/`.ssh` are each a 404.

### The estimate is no longer computed on a polled request, and finding 107 is confirmed flaky

`test_run_stop::test_stopping_a_producing_run_cancels_the_node_in_flight` failed after the ETA
work with `activity Heartbeat timeout`. Reverting `run_view` to HEAD made it pass, so it looked
settled: my change, my regression. **That conclusion was wrong, from one sample.** Neutering the
ETA while keeping every other change still failed, and HEAD itself then went **fail, fail, pass**
over three runs on a box at load average 1.44. The test is flaky on its own — finding 107, now
seen in both directions — and the box being "quiet" is not the discriminator it was recorded as.

The investigation was still worth it, because measuring the cost found two real defects:

* **410 ms of work sat inside a view polled every two seconds.** `dag_edges` on the line below it
  is already deliberately pushed off the event loop with the comment "the run views poll this
  every couple of seconds", and the estimate walked straight past that. The breakdown is the
  interesting part — of 410 ms, finding the reports is 12 ms and parsing all 226 is **5 ms**, while
  **376 ms is loading the 16 lane definitions** that `infer_workflow` needs. `run_view` now asks
  `durations.is_warm()` and, when cold, schedules the read in a thread and returns `eta: null` for
  that one poll. One poll without an estimate is nothing.
* **`rglob("run.json")` walked the media to find the reports** — 51,548 directory entries for 226
  files, 59 ms against 12 ms for five bounded patterns. The output tree grows without bound and
  the reports do not, so that gap only widens.

And it exposed a third thing that was simply missing: nothing ever re-read the history, so a
long-lived API server would have answered with the medians it read at startup forever. The cache
is now a plain dict of `root -> (built_at, table)` rather than an `lru_cache`, because a request
path needs two things `lru_cache` cannot give: asking whether a read is free *before* paying for
it, and swapping a rebuild in **whole**. Clearing and refilling leaves a 410 ms window answering
"no estimate", which on a two-second poll is a visible flicker; `rebuild()` builds first and
assigns second, so the old answer keeps serving. Stale after five minutes, rebuilt in the
background.

```
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_run_eta.py   -> 23 passed  (4 new: is_warm/is_stale, the gapless
                                              swap, staleness, and the bounded report search)
test_run_stop -k in_flight, at HEAD        -> fail, fail, pass   (finding 107, load average 1.44)
just test                                  -> exit 0
uv run pytest -q -m integration            -> exit 0   55 passed, 1433 deselected  (5m31s,
                                              test_run_stop included, passing this time)
just lint / just typecheck                 -> 0 errors
```

## Phase — "when i started the run with claude i want claude to review, and review every image for consistency"

Both review stages park on a person because passing measurements is necessary and never
sufficient: the faults this pipeline actually produced were two characters back to back, four
figures where two were staged, "honey-coloured" drawn as jars of honey. None of that is
measurable. But a run started from a Claude Code session has an agent sitting right there, and
**27 runs on this machine were parked at `review_frames`** with their drawings finished and nobody
coming.

**The trigger is the real one.** `CLAUDECODE=1` is set in every command a Claude Code session
runs, so `qc/reviewer.py` detects "I started this run with Claude" rather than needing a flag
somebody remembers. Order is explicit node parameter, then `CF_REVIEWER`, then the environment,
then `operator` — and an unrecognised value **falls through to `operator` instead of raising**,
because the failure mode has to be "a person is asked" and never "nobody is". `--as
overnight-review` once wrote a verdict that the next `review_frames` refused; a typo must not be
able to skip a gate.

**Every pair, not just neighbours.** `continuity_finding` compares consecutive frames, which
answers "was there a cut here" and cannot answer "do all six belong to one set" — a set can drift
a little at each step and end somewhere else with every consecutive pair looking fine. So
`consistency_matrix` compares all pairs (15 for a six-picture story) on a coarse
saturation-weighted hue histogram plus a luma histogram, and gives each frame its **median
distance to the others**, which is what identifies the odd one out. Written whole to
`reviews/frames/consistency.json` so a reviewer can cite the number instead of an impression.

**The threshold was measured, and the first guess was wrong in the direction that matters.** 0.22
looked reasonable and would have flagged nothing:

```
six views of one pinecone (ps1c-pinecone)      0.033 - 0.046 per frame
six views of one whelk (ps3c-whelk)            0.033 - 0.053 per frame
six views of one amber (ps2c-amber)            0.020 median — the LOWEST of the three
one whelk drawing dropped into the pinecone set  0.140   <- a real intruder
two frames from genuinely different runs       0.111 - 0.167 pairwise
```

So **0.11**: twice the worst legitimate reading, a fifth below a real intruder. The amber entry is
the one to remember — that set's palette went *uniformly tan*, a fault obvious by eye, and it
scores as the most consistent set of the three, because it was. Low distance means "one world",
never "good".

**"Everything is an outlier" is not a finding.** The first version flagged all six frames of
`w-iceberg` — an `image-set` of six deliberately different viewpoints, median 0.243. An outlier is
only meaningful against a set that agrees with itself, so when the *typical* pair is already past
the threshold the report says `spread: true` and names **no** individual frame. A set is allowed
to be varied on purpose; what it cannot hide is one frame that left the others behind.

**The agent has to look, and that is enforced rather than trusted.** `--accept-all` is a reviewer
saying yes to a batch nobody opened — defensible for a person reading one contact sheet, not for
an agent that reads images one at a time. So it is **refused for `--as agent`**, and the verdict
must name every frame in `--accept`/`--reject`; the CLI lists the ones left undecided. The gate
writes `reviews/frames/request.md` for an agent: every image by path, every measurement, and the
command that answers.

**Used it, and it caught something the numbers passed.** `m09-audio-picture-orrery`, six frames,
consistency **0.0945 median with no outliers** — a pass:

* four frames draw an oil lamp on a cellar table, two a handled brass urn. **No orrery anywhere**,
  and the set is not even wrong consistently.
* four draw a border or a picture frame, against an explicit "no borders" — two are framed
  *pictures of a scene*.
* one is **photographic**, with real depth-of-field bokeh on the background pebbles, against "no
  photographic lighting" and a flat-wash watercolour style.

Palette and tone agree across two different objects, exactly as the module's docstring says they
would. All six rejected with that reason recorded. Then the accept path end to end on
`p1-pinecone-set`: read all six (one pine cone, one light, drifting as a sequence should), 0.0652
median and no outliers with looking in agreement, accepted by name — and the gate let the run
through `package_sequence`, `qc_deliverable` and `compile_destination_packages` to `passed: true`.

`human_gate_stages()` now has three callers from one definition (`make`'s GATE-vs-FAIL label, the
run history's `review` outcome, the compiler's executor), and `review_frames` gained a `reviewer`
combo on the canvas — blank means "let the run decide".

**Commands run:**

```
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_reviews.py       -> 26 passed (12 new)
just test                                      -> exit 0   1438 passed, 1 skipped
just test-integration                          -> 54 passed, 1 failed: test_run_stop
                                                  ..._cancels_the_node_in_flight, the flake proven
                                                  above to fail at HEAD too (fail, fail, pass)
just schemas                                   -> node_catalog.json regenerated (reviewer widget)
content-factory make ... --from frames_gate     -> gate wrote request.md + consistency.json
frames review --as agent --accept-all           -> refused, as designed
frames review --as agent --accept <one of six>  -> refused, naming the 5 undecided
frames review --as agent --reject <all six>     -> verdict recorded (m09 orrery)
frames review --as agent --accept <all six>     -> passed; run completed to destination packages
```

**Using it found a bug in it, of a shape this command has now had twice.** The first real
rejection reason ran past `reason`'s 400 characters, and `model_copy(update=...)` **does not
re-validate in Pydantic v2** — so the CLI wrote a `verdict.json` its own contract could not read
back, and the next `review_frames` died on the file it had just been handed. The comment already
in this command records the first instance of the same shape (`--as overnight-review` wrote a
verdict the gate then refused). The verdict is now assembled through
`FrameReviewBatch.model_validate` before anything is written, so a violated constraint is a
sentence at the boundary instead of a crash three stages later:

```
  frames.3.reason: String should have at most 400 characters
nothing was written. Shorten it and run again.
```

**The backlog, measured the way the gate measures it** — merging `verdict.json` onto `batch.json`
and honouring the digest match, because `batch.json` alone lags a verdict and reads 31:

```
awaiting review: 12 runs / 71 images        decided by agent: 26        unreadable verdicts: 0
```

`just schemas-check` reports drift on `fixtures/schema/node_catalog.json` until that regenerated
fixture is committed — it gates by `git diff`, and the only change in it is the reviewer widget.

**Compose vanished mid-phase.** Both `postgres` and `temporal` were *removed*, not stopped
(`docker inspect` → "no such object"), while every other project's containers stayed up, so
something ran a `compose down`; the integration suite answered 55 skipped rather than failing,
which is the honest behaviour but easy to read as a pass. `just up` and `just migrate` restored
it. Worth knowing that a skipped integration suite looks like a green one at a glance, and that
another session sharing this checkout can take the database away underneath a gate.

Not done, and not pretended: `review_assets` still takes a free-text `--as <name>` and writes
`approved_by`, a different contract from the frame gate's `ReviewerKind`. Nothing is parked there,
so routing it was left rather than guessed at.

## Phase — "the history window on the left, and review inside the workspace" (2026-09-10, evening)

> *"change the like history window to be on the left side and for the images waiting for review and
> all of that to all be in the same menu inside the workspace. so you never need to move from that
> screen to review and so on. you should easily be able to switch between previous runs."*

**The gate could only be answered from a terminal, and that is why 12 runs are parked at it.** The
history shipped this morning as a window over the canvas: open it, pick a run, look, close it. The
frame-review gate — the thing those runs are actually waiting for — was not in the app at all. To
answer it you needed the run's path on disk and `content-factory frames review`. So the pictures
that most needed a person were the hardest to reach from the screen the person works on.

**What moved.** History is now a third tab in the left dock beside Nodes and Models (the dock
widens to 21rem for it, back to 15rem under 900px), the toolbar button carries the count of
drawings waiting (`History 71 to review`), and opening a run opens it **over the canvas**
(`RunPane`) with the list still on the left — switching runs is one click and the graph keeps its
scroll, selection and pending edits underneath. "Needs review" is its own filter, and it counts
*drawings nobody has decided about*, not runs whose last stage failed: a run whose frames were all
rejected is parked at the same gate and what it needs is a redraw, not a reviewer.

**The gate is now answerable in the browser, through the same rules as the CLI.** New route
`/v1/run-history/{run_id}/review` — GET returns every frame with its measurements and the file to
look at; POST records the verdict. Reading needs `viewer`, recording needs the `reviewer` role that
existed for exactly this, and every verdict is audited as `review.frames` with what it decided.

The rules did **not** get a second implementation. `qc/verdict.py` now owns `decide()` (unknown
ids, a frame in both lists, an agent's blanket accept, frames an agent left undecided, and the
contract check that stopped a 401-character reason reaching disk) and `merge_verdict()` (a verdict
applies only to the images it reviewed, by digest). Three callers: the CLI, the review panel's
service, and `stage_review_frames` itself. That last one matters — the panel has to show exactly
what the gate will decide on, and two copies of a digest comparison is how it would come to show
something else. `--accept-all` is still refused for an agent **through the API too**, with a test
that proves it, because a second door must not open a wider one.

**What a verdict does and does not do, said in the UI.** It unblocks the gate; it starts nothing.
The panel prints the command that continues the run (`run-local <lane> --project-dir <dir> --from
review_frames`) beside the result rather than implying a film is now being made.

**The backlog, and every drawing in it resolves to a file.** Each history row carries
`awaiting_review`, computed by merging `verdict.json` onto `batch.json` — 13 ms for 100 runs, on
top of a listing that already walks the tree:

```
12 runs with drawings waiting / 71 images     frames whose file is missing: 0     stale digests: 0
```

`frame:0007` is `sequence/frames/0007.png`; `shot_ab54…:0000` comes from the anchor manifest, read
rather than guessed, because a lane may write more than one frame per shot. Each frame is hashed
against the batch, so a picture regenerated after the gate ran is labelled instead of silently
offered for approval.

**Found while verifying: `apps/web` has not built since an earlier session.** `just test`,
`just lint` and `just typecheck` were all green, and `pnpm build` failed — only lightningcss
minification parses the CSS strictly. Two defects, both at HEAD, neither mine:

* `workspace.css` — a mangled comment had swallowed `.cf-templates__grid`'s selector and its
  `grid-auto-rows/gap/padding/align-content`, and spliced the block-cards comment into the middle
  of another one. The templates panel had silently lost the scroll fix its own comment describes.
* `store.css:61` — `background: var(--cf-panel-2));`, a stray parenthesis.

Both repaired; the production build is green. Worth remembering that the core suite never builds
the app, so a CSS syntax error is invisible to it.

**A screenshot found the one layout bug the tests could not.** In the run row, cost and time were
pushed apart with `margin-right: 3.5rem` — a guess that holds at 30rem and fails at 21rem, where
`7m` and `Wed 03:12` rendered as `7mWed 03:12`. They now share one flex cell.

**Commands run:**

```
just typecheck                                  -> exit 0
just lint                                       -> exit 0 (after ruff N818: VerdictRefusedError)
just fmt                                        -> 3 files reformatted
just test                                       -> exit 0  1441 passed, 1 skipped (py)
                                                   + 25 web files / 134 tests, all TS suites pass
just test-integration                           -> 61 passed, 1448 deselected
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_reviews.py        -> 29 passed (3 new: both lane shapes, a redrawn
                                                   frame becoming a question again, agent refusal)
uv run pytest tests/api/test_frame_review_api.py -> 6 passed (new)
pnpm --filter web build                         -> exit 0 after the two CSS repairs (was failing)
playwright screenshot of the dock + run pane     -> 1600x1000 and 1000x900, the overlap above
```

`just schemas-check` still reports drift on `fixtures/schema/node_catalog.json` — the regenerated
fixture from the previous phase, uncommitted; nothing here touched a contract.

**Not done, and not pretended.** Recording a verdict does not resume the run (no local-run control
plane in the API — the command is shown instead). The Assets page still lives outside the workspace;
it reads the image-sequence output root, a different source from the run history, so folding it in
was left rather than guessed at. `review_assets` still takes a free-text `--as <name>`, unchanged.

## 2026-09-11 — the repo reads itself again, and a node can be frozen (session auto-content-n8n)

Started as a question about whether n8n makes this repo redundant. It does not — n8n's execution
engine alone is 16,729 lines against this repo's 8,896 of orchestration, and one `Wait` node costs
647 lines to park a run — but reading it turned up patterns worth taking, and reading *this* repo
while answering turned up three things that were simply wrong.

**STATUS.md had stopped being a status file.** 8,032 lines, 650 KB, roughly 160k tokens, while
CLAUDE.md's first instruction is to read it first. "Resume instruction (next smallest task)" sat at
line 487 with 7,500 lines of session journal after it. It is now 216 lines — checklist, decisions,
ledger, limitations, next task — and this journal is where the proof and the story go. All 56
sections are accounted for: 5 kept, 3 to `2026-08.md`, 48 here. CLAUDE.md's phase rule now says so.

**The README claimed phases 3-13 were unimplemented** while the checklist marked them GREEN. That
section predated their landing and was never updated. It now names what is actually undone — all
three operator-gated — and leads with the honest one: nothing this repo produces has ever been
published anywhere. "What works now" also stopped at 2026-09-01 and missed the workflow catalogue,
the node-graph workspace, the local AI stack, the audio chain, Blender scene control and run
observability.

**`just test` could not see a broken build.** lint, typecheck and 1,441 tests all passed on the CSS
that lightningcss then refused to minify — which is how `.cf-templates__grid` lost its selector to a
mangled comment and stayed broken across sessions. `pnpm -r run build` is now part of the recipe.

**A widget can say when it applies.** `WidgetSpec.displayOptions`, taken from n8n's shape: `show` is
a conjunction, `hide` a disjunction, `hide` wins. `restore_speech` showed `enhancer_mode` and
`enhancer_nfe` whether or not an enhancer was on, though both are read only inside
`if spec.enhancer == "resemble_enhance"`; `generate_video` showed three GGUF filenames read on the
LTX branch only; `plan_shots` carried "(planner: fixture)" inside a placeholder because the widget
had no way to say it itself. Two properties matter more than the rendering: a hidden widget keeps
its value, and a hidden widget stops being required — a control nobody can see must never block a
graph. n8n's model cannot express "any of these three is not off", so `restore_speech.device` stays
visible and the comment says why rather than inventing a condition language.

**A node can be pinned.** `--from` is the right instrument for "carry on from where it stopped" and
the wrong one for how this pipeline is actually iterated: the expensive nodes are near the front
(`generate_anchor`, `synthesize_narration`, `generate_video`) and the node being worked on is near
the back, so `--from` means naming the tail every time and a full run means paying minutes of GPU
time for pictures nobody is looking at. A pin says the other thing — this node is frozen, never
execute it — so the front can be pinned once and the tail run flat out.

Three rules keep it from being a way to lie about a run:

- **Pinning something that never ran is refused.** There would be no output to reuse, and a pin that
  quietly means "skip and hope" is the failure mode the whole module exists to avoid.
- **A stale pin refuses before the run spends anything**, not six minutes in when a later stage
  cannot find what it needed.
- **A pinned node is visible and recorded as pinned.** It logs `pin <node> <hash>, not run` through
  the same terse path every other outcome uses — the first version set the current node and printed
  nothing, so a pinned step simply vanished from the output, which is the one thing it must not do —
  and lands in `run.json` with `pinned: true`, so provenance reads "frozen, not produced".
  `services/durations.py` skips those records: a skipped stage took 0.0 s, and letting that sample
  in would teach the estimator that a six-minute generation is instant.

**Commands run:**

```
just fmt                                  -> exit 0
just lint                                 -> exit 0
just typecheck                            -> exit 0
just test                                 -> 1449 passed, 1 skipped (py, was 1441)
                                             + all TS suites; node-graph 49 -> 58 tests
                                             + pnpm -r run build, now part of the recipe
just test-integration                     -> 61 passed
just schemas                              -> regenerated; widget_display present on
                                             generate_video, plan_shots, restore_speech
content-factory make silent-video --until shots            -> 2 stages, report written
content-factory pins add silent-video story                -> story (plan_story, fae37b975104)
content-factory make silent-video --until shots            -> pin plan_story, not run; plan_shots ok
content-factory make silent-video --until shots --no-pins  -> both stages ran
content-factory make silent-video --until shots --pin shots -> both nodes pinned, neither ran
```

**Found while doing it.** Two test doubles for `run_plan` had frozen at an older signature and broke
the moment the real one grew `use_pins`; both now mirror it. `just schemas-check` compares against
git rather than the filesystem, so a correct-but-uncommitted generated file reads as drift — the
"drift" carried since the previous session was exactly that, and committing it cleared it.

**Not done, and not pretended.** Pinning is the operator-facing half of what n8n calls partial
execution; the other half — a dirty-node walk that works out the minimal set to re-run from a graph
rather than from a linear step list — is not here, and the lane is still executed as an ordered list.
Nothing about the three operator gates changed: **still nothing has ever been published**.

### The drift knobs were reachable from one door only (2026-09-11, later)

Checking whether the catalogue's declared defaults agree with what the stages actually default to
turned up something narrower and worse. `stage_generate_keyframes` reads `drift_profile`,
`locked_min` and `style_delta_max`, and its own comment tells the operator to reach for
`--set spokes.drift_profile=uncalibrated` — but the node declared none of them. So they were
settable from the command line, invisible on the canvas, and **refused by `catalog.py` if a lane
tried to freeze one**. The failure this repo already recorded is exactly that shape: an `image-set`
resumed to redraw a single frame lost the `--set`, met the mock-calibrated 0.92 that no real frame
reaches, and was BLOCKED after three attempts at a measured 0.8491.

Two doors, one lock. `workflows/catalog.py` rejects a definition that sets a widget the node does
not declare, for the reason its docstring gives — "a typo'd key is silently swallowed by the
stage's `_param` default and the workflow quietly does something else". `--set` checked the node or
stage name and never the key, so `--set spokes.drift_profil=uncalibrated` was accepted, ignored,
and the run gated on the very threshold the override was meant to lift. It now refuses, and names
what it would have accepted.

The three knobs are declared. `locked_min` and `style_delta_max` default to 0 meaning *leave the
profile alone*, because the real thresholds are per-style and per-camera and resolved at run time;
a widget carrying a number would be a third calibration to keep in step, and its default would
silently override the other two on every node that never touched it. The stage reads them as
`_param_float(...) or profile_value` to match.

`widget_audit.undeclared_reads()` is the mirror of the audit that already existed. `unread_widgets`
catches a declared widget nothing reads — a control that looks bound and does nothing.
`undeclared_reads` catches the opposite, which is worse, because it is reachable from `--set` and
from nowhere else. It reports eleven more across eight stages and they are **untriaged**: two
legitimate reasons to read an undeclared key are already in the code — the runner injecting one
from a dedicated flag (`resolved_steps` writes `story`, `subject`, `planner`, `fixture_path` from
`--story`, `--subject`, `--shots`) and a stage injecting one itself before reading it back
(`stage_lock_generation` takes `style` and `model` from the anchor's recorded marker so a lock can
never name art direction the anchor was not drawn in). Telling those from a real gap needs reading
each one, so the test pins the current set rather than asserting it empty: the backlog can shrink
deliberately and cannot grow by accident.

**Commands run:**

```
just fmt / lint / typecheck                                   -> exit 0
just test                                                     -> 1455 passed, 1 skipped
                                                                 (1449 -> 1455: +5 overrides, +1 audit)
make image-set --plan --set spokes.drift_profil=...           -> refused, names the four declared
make image-set --plan --set spokes.drift_profile=uncalibrated -> lands on the node
just schemas                                                  -> generate_keyframes now declares
                                                                 model, drift_profile, locked_min,
                                                                 style_delta_max
```

**Not done.** The eleven undeclared reads are recorded, not resolved.
